\displaystyle\epsilon^{-3}\frac{1}{2}I^{-1}Q\left[\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}\left(I(\epsilon\xi)\right)+\overline{\epsilon}(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{I(\epsilon\xi)}\left[I(\epsilon h)\right]+\mathcal{O}(\overline{\epsilon}^{2})\right].

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle d\mathfrak{G}_{\epsilon}(\xi)[h]

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{-3}\frac{1}{2}I^{-1}Q\left[(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{I(\epsilon\xi)+q_{\perp}(I(\epsilon\xi))}\left[I(\epsilon h)+(dq_{\perp})_{I(\epsilon\xi)}[I(\epsilon h)]\right]\right]

\displaystyle-\epsilon^{-3}\frac{1}{2}I^{-1}Q\left[(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{I(\epsilon\xi)+\mathfrak{F}^{(2)}(I(\epsilon\xi))}\left[I(\epsilon h)+(d\mathfrak{F}^{(2)})_{I(\epsilon\xi)}[I(\epsilon h)]\right]\right]

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{-3}\frac{1}{2}I^{-1}Q\left[(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{I(\epsilon\xi)+q_{\perp}(I(\epsilon\xi))}\left[I(\epsilon h)+(dq_{\perp})_{I(\epsilon\xi)}[I(\epsilon h)]\right]\right]

\displaystyle-\epsilon^{-3}\frac{1}{2}I^{-1}Q\left[(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{I(\epsilon\xi)}\left[I(\epsilon h)\right]\right].

Recall that, according to Lemma
2.6
together with (
2.14
), we have

\displaystyle\|q_{\perp}(I(\epsilon\xi))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|I(\epsilon\xi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}^{2}\simeq\epsilon^{2}|\xi|_{s+1}^{2}\simeq\epsilon^{2}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}^{2},

\displaystyle\|q_{\perp}(I(\epsilon\xi))-\mathfrak{F}^{(2)}(I(\epsilon\xi))\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|I(\epsilon\xi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}^{3}\simeq\epsilon^{3}|\xi|_{s+1}^{3}\simeq\epsilon^{3}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}^{3},

\displaystyle\|d\left(q_{\perp}-\mathfrak{F}^{(2)}\right)_{(I(\epsilon\xi))}[I(\epsilon h)]\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|I(\epsilon\xi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}^{2}\|I(\epsilon h)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\simeq\epsilon^{2}|\xi|_{s+1}^{2}\epsilon|h|_{s+1}\simeq\epsilon^{3}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}},

\displaystyle\|\left(dq_{\perp}\right)_{(I(\epsilon\xi))}[I(\epsilon h)]\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|I(\epsilon\xi)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\|I(\epsilon h)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\simeq\epsilon|\xi|_{s+1}\epsilon|h|_{s+1}\simeq\epsilon^{2}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}},

for all
\xi\in l_{s+3}^{2}
and
h\in l_{s+3}^{2}
. Now, we set

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=I(\epsilon\xi)+q_{\perp}(I(\epsilon\xi)),

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=I(\epsilon\xi)+\mathfrak{F}^{(2)}(I(\epsilon\xi)),

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=I(\epsilon h)+(dq_{\perp})_{I(\epsilon\xi)}[I(\epsilon h)],

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=I(\epsilon h)+(d\mathfrak{F}^{(2)})_{I(\epsilon\xi)}[I(\epsilon h)]

and Lemma
4.5
yields

\displaystyle\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{X}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[V\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}=

\displaystyle\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{X}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[U\right]+(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[V\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{X}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[U\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}+\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[V\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left((d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{X}-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\right)\left[U\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}+\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(2)})_{Y}\left[U-V\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|X-Y\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\left\|U\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}+\left\|Y\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\left\|U-V\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\epsilon^{3}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}^{3}\cdot\epsilon\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}}+\epsilon\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\cdot\epsilon^{3}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\lesssim\epsilon^{4}\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}},

for all
\xi\in l_{s+3}^{2}
and
h\in l_{s+3}^{2}
. Similarly, we set

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=I(\epsilon\xi),

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle=I(\epsilon h),

and Lemma
4.5
yields

\displaystyle\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{X}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[W\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}=

\displaystyle\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{X}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[U\right]+(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[W\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{X}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[U\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}+\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[U\right]-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[W\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left((d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{X}-(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\right)\left[U\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}+\left\|(d\mathfrak{f}^{(3)})_{Z}\left[U-W\right]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|X-Z\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}^{2}\left\|U\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}+\left\|Z\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}^{2}\left\|U-W\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\epsilon^{2}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}\right)^{2}\cdot\epsilon\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}}+\left(\epsilon\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\right)^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2}\|\xi\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\lesssim\epsilon^{4}\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}},

for all
\xi\in l_{s+3}^{2}
and
h\in l_{s+3}^{2}
. Putting all together, yields

\displaystyle\|d_{\xi}\mathfrak{G}_{\epsilon}(\xi)[h]\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\lesssim\epsilon\|h\|_{\mathcal{Q}},

for all
\xi\in l_{s+3}^{2}
and
h\in l_{s+3}^{2}
, that completes the proof.
∎

It remains to show that there exists a solution to (
2.19
), that is

\displaystyle\pm\mathfrak{A}\xi+\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}\rangle(\xi)=-\left(\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)+\mathfrak{G}_{\epsilon}(\xi)\pm\frac{2\epsilon^{2}}{\ssomega^{2}-1}\mathfrak{R}_{\epsilon}(\xi,\ssomega)\right).

that we rewrite as

\displaystyle{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi)=\mathfrak{H}_{\epsilon}(\xi),

(2.22)

where we introduced the
modified operator

\displaystyle{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\pm\mathfrak{A}\xi+\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}\rangle(\xi)+\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)

and set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}_{\epsilon}(\xi):=\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)-\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)-\mathfrak{G}_{\epsilon}(\xi)\mp\frac{2\epsilon^{2}}{\ssomega^{2}-1}\mathfrak{R}_{\epsilon}(\xi,\ssomega).

Note that Lemmata
2.10
,
2.11
and the smoothness
7
7

7

In Lemma
2.13
we show that
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)
is smooth with respect to
\epsilon
. As one can see in the proof of Lemma
2.13
,
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)
is in fact linear with respect to
\epsilon
. See (
2.27
).
of
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)
with respect to
\epsilon
yield

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{H}_{\epsilon}(\xi)\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\lesssim\epsilon,\hskip 5.0pt\|d_{\xi}\mathfrak{H}_{\epsilon}(\xi)\|_{\mathcal{Q}}\lesssim\epsilon.

The following result constitutes the main modification of Bambusi–Paleari’s theorem.

Lemma 2.12

(Solution to the
Q-
equation)
.

Let
\xi_{0}\in l_{s+3}^{2}
be a non–degenerate zero of the modified operator

\displaystyle{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(x)

\displaystyle=\pm\mathfrak{A}x+\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}\rangle(x)+\mathfrak{F}_{0}(x),

that is

\displaystyle{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi_{0})=0,\hskip 5.0pt\ker\left(d{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi_{0})\right)=\{0\}.

Then, there exists a positive
\epsilon_{0}
and a Lipschitz map

\displaystyle\xi:[0,\epsilon_{0})\longrightarrow l_{s+3}^{2},\hskip 5.0pt\epsilon\longmapsto\xi(\epsilon)

that solves the
Q-
equation (
2.18
) with the plus sign.
Furthermore, we have the estimate

\displaystyle|\xi(\epsilon)-\xi_{0}|_{s+3}\lesssim\epsilon.

Proof.

The proof follows from the implicit function theorem and is similar to the one of Proposition 4.8 in Bambusi–Paleari
[
2
]
. Let
\xi_{0}\in l_{s+3}^{2}
be a non–degenerate zero of the modified operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\pm}
and define the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}:\mathbb{R}\times l_{s+3}^{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{s}^{1},\hskip 5.0pt(\epsilon,\xi)\longmapsto\mathcal{G}(\epsilon,\xi):={\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi)-\mathfrak{H}_{\epsilon}(\xi)

and note that it is Lipschitz, differentiable at
\epsilon=0
and it vanishes at
(\epsilon,\xi)=(0,\xi_{0})
. It remains to show that its differential with respect to
\xi
at
(0,\xi_{0})
,

\displaystyle d\mathcal{G}(0,\xi_{0}):l_{s+3}^{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{s}^{1},\hskip 5.0ptX\longmapsto d\mathcal{G}(0,\xi_{0})(X)=d{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi_{0})(X),

is an isomorphism. Equivalently, this means that, for all
Y\in\mathcal{H}_{s}^{1}
, there exists
X\in l_{s+3}^{2}
that solves the equation

\displaystyle d{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi_{0})(X)=Y.

Now, the operator

\displaystyle d{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi_{0})=\pm\mathfrak{A}+d\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}\rangle(\xi_{0})+d\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi_{0})

is a Fredholm operator since it is the sum of a Fredholm and a compact operator due to the fact that
\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}
and
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)
are bounded on
l_{s+3}^{2}
, and that they are differentiable with bounded differential. Since the defect index of
d{\swabfamily M}_{\pm}(\xi_{0})
is
0
from the non–degeneracy condition, it follows that it is an isomorphism, and thus we can apply the implicit function theorem. Note finally, that the range of
\epsilon
, defined by
\epsilon_{0}
, does not depend on
\ssomega
, since
\mathcal{G}
depends continuously on
\ssomega
and all the necessary bounds hold uniformly with respect to
\ssomega
.
∎

Finally, we prove Theorem
2.5
.

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

Let
\ssomega\in\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}
be fixed. Then, according to Lemmata
2.6
and
2.12
, there exists
\epsilon_{0}>0
so that the map

\displaystyle[0,\epsilon_{0})\ni\epsilon\longmapsto\left(\epsilon I(\xi(\epsilon)),q_{\perp}\left(\epsilon I(\xi(\epsilon))\right)\right)

solves both the P–equation (
2.10
) and the Q–equation (
2.11
). Furthermore, pick
\ssomega_{\star}
so that
\epsilon(\ssomega_{\star})=\epsilon_{0}
. Then, the function

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}(\ssomega):=\pm\frac{\ssomega^{2}-1}{2}

solves (
2.12
) and the map

\displaystyle\ssomega\longmapsto\left(\epsilon I(\xi(\epsilon(\ssomega))),q_{\perp}\left(\epsilon I(\xi(\epsilon(\ssomega)))\right)\right)

defines a family of solutions to (
2.9
) labelled by
\ssomega\in\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\cap[1,1+\ssomega_{\star}]
or
\ssomega\in\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\cap[1-\ssomega_{\star},1]
. Finally, the map
\epsilon\longmapsto\epsilon(\ssomega)
is one–to–one and hence this family can be also parametrized by
\epsilon\in\mathcal{E}_{\gamma}=\epsilon(\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\cap[1,1+\ssomega_{\star}])
or
\epsilon\in\mathcal{E}_{\gamma}=\epsilon(\mathcal{W}_{\alpha}\cap[1-\ssomega_{\star},1])
that completes the proof.
∎

2.6.
The function
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)

For future reference, we compute

•

\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)
for general initial data (Lemma
2.13
),

•

\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)
for small
\epsilon
close to zero and 1–mode initial data (Lemma
2.14
),

•

d\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)
at the 1–mode initial data (Lemma
2.15
).

To begin with, we compute
\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)
for general initial data.

Lemma 2.13

(Computation of
\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)
for general initial data)
.

Let
\xi=\{\xi^{m}:m\geq 0\}\in l_{s+3}^{2}
. Then, for all integers
m\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)\right)^{m}=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ij\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ij\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0).

In addition, the function
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}
is smooth with respect to
\epsilon
.

Proof.

Let
\xi=\{\xi^{m}:m\geq 0\}\in l_{s+3}^{2}
be any initial data,
\epsilon>0
, set
x=\epsilon\xi
and pick any integer
m\geq 0
. Then, we compute

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\{u^{k}:k\geq 0\}\right)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-3\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}u^{i}u^{j},

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-3\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)^{i}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)^{j}

\displaystyle=-3\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}x^{i}x^{j}\cos(\ssomega_{i}t)\cos(\ssomega_{j}t)

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}x^{i}x^{j}\cos((\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})t)-\frac{3}{2}\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}x^{i}x^{j}\cos((\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})t).

Then,
(P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x)))^{m}
is given by

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}x^{i}x^{j}\cos((\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})t)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}x^{i}x^{j}\cos((\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})t)\right]

and
(L_{\ssomega}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x)))^{m}
reads

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}\left[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}}{\lambda_{m,\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}}}x^{i}x^{j}\cos((\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})t)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}}{\lambda_{m,\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}}}x^{i}x^{j}\cos((\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})t)\right],

where we used the fact that the eigenvalues of
L_{\ssomega}
are given by
\lambda_{ml}=\ssomega_{m}^{2}-l^{2}\ssomega^{2}
. Hence, using the above together with the symmetries of the Fourier coefficients
\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}=\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\nu\kappa m}
for all integers
\kappa,\nu,m\geq 0
, we deduce that
(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))))^{m}
is given by

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))\right)^{\kappa}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))\right)^{\nu}=

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left[\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)^{\kappa}+\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))\right)^{\kappa}\right]\left[\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)^{\nu}+\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))\right)^{\nu}\right]=

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)^{\kappa}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)^{\nu}-6\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))\right)^{\nu}

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(x))\right)^{\nu}

and, by setting
x=\epsilon\xi
, we infer that
(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi))))^{m}
equals to

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}+\epsilon^{3}\left(E(\xi)\right)^{m}+\epsilon^{4}\left(F(\xi)\right)^{m},

(2.23)

where

\displaystyle\left(E(\xi)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-6\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(\xi))\right)^{\nu},

\displaystyle\left(F(\xi)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(\xi))\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(\xi))\right)^{\nu}.

In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left(E(\xi)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-6\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(\xi))\right)^{\nu}

(2.24)

\displaystyle=-6\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\xi^{\kappa}\cos(\ssomega_{\kappa}t)\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\pPhi^{t}(\xi))\right)^{\nu}

(2.25)

\displaystyle=\left(E(\xi)\right)_{-}^{m}+\left(E(\xi)\right)_{+}^{m},

(2.26)

where we set

\displaystyle\left(E(\xi)\right)_{-}^{m}

\displaystyle=9\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}}{\ssomega_{m}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}\cos((\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})t)\cos(\ssomega_{\kappa}t),

\displaystyle\left(E(\xi)\right)_{+}^{m}

\displaystyle=9\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}}{\ssomega_{m}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}\cos((\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})t)\cos(\ssomega_{\kappa}t).

Next, we firstly apply the linear flow and then the average in time to obtain

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=\epsilon^{-3}\left(\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left((\cdot)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\cdot)\right)\rangle(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{-3}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)\right)^{m}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)\right)^{m}dt+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(E(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt+\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(F(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{-1}\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\rangle(\xi)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(E(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt+\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(F(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(E(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt+\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(F(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt,

(2.27)

where we used the condition that
\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}
is non–resonant according to (
2.7
). Then, the latter at
\epsilon=0
boils down to

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(E(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(E(\xi)\right)^{m}\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(E(\xi)\right)_{-}^{m}\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(E(\xi)\right)_{+}^{m}\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt.

Finally, we use the fact that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos((\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})t)\cos(\ssomega_{\kappa}t)\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\pm}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos((\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m})t)dt=\frac{\pi}{2}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

to compute

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(E(\xi)\right)_{-}^{m}\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{2\pi}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}}{\ssomega_{m}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos((\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})t)\cos(\ssomega_{\kappa}t)\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}}{\ssomega_{m}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0).

Similarly, we infer

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(E(\xi)\right)_{+}^{m}\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{m}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}}{\ssomega_{m}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0),

that completes the proof.
∎

Next, we compute
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)
for small
\epsilon
close to zero and 1–mode initial data.

Lemma 2.14

(Computation of
\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)
for small
\epsilon
close to zero and 1–mode initial data)
.

Assume that
\ssomega_{n}=n+1
, for all integers
n\geq 0
. Let
\gamma\geq 0
be an integer,
\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi
be the 1–mode initial data, that is

\displaystyle\xi^{m}:=\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}\mathds{1}(m=\gamma),\hskip 5.0ptm\geq 0.

Then, for all integers
m\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)\right)^{m}=\mathfrak{q}_{\gamma}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\mathds{1}(m=\gamma)+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon),

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\gamma}:=\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu=0}^{2\gamma}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}+\frac{\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}\neq(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2})}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right)

Proof.

Let
\gamma\geq 0
be an integer, define
\xi
as the 1–mode initial data, that is

\displaystyle\xi^{m}:=\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}\mathds{1}(m=\gamma),\hskip 5.0ptm\geq 0.

and pick any integer
m\geq 0
. Then, we compute

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\{u^{k}:k\geq 0\}\right)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-3\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}u^{i}u^{j},

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-3\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)^{i}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)^{j}=-3\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}\cos(\ssomega_{i}t)\cos(\ssomega_{j}t)

\displaystyle=-3\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma m}\cos^{2}(\ssomega_{\gamma}t)=-\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma m}\left[1+\cos(2\ssomega_{\gamma}t)\right].

Recall the definition of the eigenvalues
\ssomega_{i}:=i+2
, for all integers
i\geq 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\ssomega_{m}\neq 0\Longleftrightarrow m\geq 0,\hskip 5.0pt\ssomega_{m}\neq 2\ssomega_{\gamma}\Longleftrightarrow m\neq 2\gamma+2.

Hence, we infer

\displaystyle\left(P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma m}\left[1+\mathds{1}(m\neq 2\gamma+2)\cos(2\ssomega_{\gamma}t)\right],

\displaystyle\left(L_{\ssomega}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma m}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{m,0}}+\mathds{1}(m\neq 2\gamma+2)\frac{1}{\lambda_{m,2\ssomega_{\gamma}}}\cos(2\ssomega_{\gamma}t)\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma m}\left[\frac{1}{\ssomega_{m}^{2}}+\frac{\mathds{1}(m\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{m}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}\ssomega^{2}}\cos(2\ssomega_{\gamma}t)\right],

where we used the fact that the eigenvalues of
L_{\ssomega}
are given by (
2.13
), i.e.
\lambda_{ml}=\ssomega_{m}^{2}-l^{2}\ssomega^{2}
. In addition, we set
x=\epsilon\xi
and
\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(x)\right)\right)\right)^{m}
is given by

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\kappa}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu}=

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left[\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\kappa}+\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\kappa}\right]\left[\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\nu}+\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu}\right]=

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\Big[\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\kappa}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\nu}+\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu}

\displaystyle+\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\nu}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\kappa}+\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu}\Big]=

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\kappa}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\nu}-6\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu}

\displaystyle-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu}=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}+\epsilon^{3}\left(E(\xi)\right)^{m}+\epsilon^{4}\left(F(\xi)\right)^{m},

where we set

\displaystyle\left(F(\xi)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=-3\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu},

\displaystyle\left(E(\xi)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=-6\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)^{\kappa}\left(L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)^{\nu}

\displaystyle=9\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\xi^{\kappa}\cos(\ssomega_{\kappa}t)\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\left[\frac{1}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}+\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\cos(2\ssomega_{\gamma}t)\right]

\displaystyle=9\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\left[\frac{1}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}\cos(\ssomega_{\gamma}t)+\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\cos(2\ssomega_{\gamma}t)\cos(\ssomega_{\gamma}t)\right]

\displaystyle=9\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right)\cos\left(\ssomega_{\gamma}t\right)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\cos\left(3\ssomega_{\gamma}t\right)\right].

Now, we firstly apply the linear flow and then the average in time to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{\epsilon}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=\epsilon^{-3}\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left((\cdot)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\cdot)\right)\rangle(\epsilon\xi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{-3}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)+L_{1}^{-1}P\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\epsilon\xi)\right)\right)\right)\right)^{m}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{-1}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)\right)^{m}dt+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(E(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt+\frac{\epsilon}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(F(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt.

On the one hand, due to (
2.7
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\left(\pPhi^{t}(\xi)\right)\right)\right)^{m}dt=\langle\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}\rangle(\xi)=0.

On the other hand, we use the fact that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos(at)\cos(bt)dt=\frac{1}{2}\mathds{1}(|a|=|b|),

for all integers
a,b\geq 0
together with

\displaystyle\ssomega_{m}=\ssomega_{\gamma}\Longleftrightarrow m=\gamma,\hskip 5.0pt\ssomega_{m}=3\ssomega_{\gamma}\Longleftrightarrow m=3\gamma+4,

to compute

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(E(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(E(\xi)\right)^{m}\cos(\ssomega_{m}t)dt

\displaystyle=9\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\Bigg[\left(\frac{1}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right)\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos\left(\ssomega_{\gamma}t\right)\cos\left(\ssomega_{m}t\right)dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\cos\left(3\ssomega_{\gamma}t\right)\cos\left(\ssomega_{m}t\right)dt\Bigg]

\displaystyle=9\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\Bigg[\left(\frac{1}{2\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right)\mathds{1}(m=\gamma)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\mathds{1}(m=3\gamma+4)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}+\frac{\mathds{1}(\nu\neq 2\gamma+2)}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right)\mathds{1}(m=\gamma)

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu\geq 0\\
\nu\neq 2\gamma+2\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,\nu,3\gamma+4}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\mathds{1}(m=3\gamma+4).

Finally, we note that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,\nu,3\gamma+4}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}=0,

for all integers
\gamma\geq 0
and
\nu\geq 0
. This follows immediately from the fact that
\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}=0
for all integers
i,j,m\geq 0
with
i+j<m
due to Lemma
5.8
below. Specifically, we have

•

\nu>2\gamma\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}=0
,

•

0\leq\nu\leq 2\gamma\Longrightarrow\gamma+\nu\leq 3\gamma<3\gamma+4\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,\nu,3\gamma+4}=0
.

Consequently, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\left(\pPhi^{t}\left(E(\xi)\right)\right)^{m}dt

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{3}\sum_{\nu=0}^{2\gamma}\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right)\mathds{1}(m=\gamma),

that completes the proof.
∎

Finally, we compute the differential of
\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)
at the 1–mode initial data.

Lemma 2.15

(Differential of
\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)
at the 1–mode initial data)
.

Let
\gamma\geq 0
be an integer,
\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi
be the 1–mode initial data, that is

\displaystyle\xi^{m}:=\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}\mathds{1}(m=\gamma),\hskip 5.0ptm\geq 0.

Then, for all
h\in l_{s+3}^{2}
and integers
m\geq 0
, we have that

\displaystyle\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\left[\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma m}h^{m}+\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\mathfrak{b}_{\gamma m}h^{2\gamma-m}\right],

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{\gamma m}

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\end{subarray}}^{m+\gamma}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{m+\gamma}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\end{subarray}}^{2\gamma}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{\gamma m}

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\end{subarray}}^{2\gamma}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{m+\gamma}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma-m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}.

Proof.

Let
\gamma\geq 0
be an integer,
\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi
to be the 1–mode initial data as above and pick any
h\in l_{s+3}^{2}
,
\epsilon>0
and integer
m\geq 0
. Then, we set
\hat{\xi}=\xi+\epsilon h
and according to Lemma
2.13
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\hat{\xi})\right)^{m}=\left(\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\hat{\xi})\right)^{m}+\left(\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\hat{\xi})\right)^{m},

where

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\hat{\xi})\right)^{m}=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ij\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\hat{\xi}^{i}\hat{\xi}^{j}\hat{\xi}^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0),

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\hat{\xi})\right)^{m}=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ij\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\hat{\xi}^{i}\hat{\xi}^{j}\hat{\xi}^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0).

We expand

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\hat{\xi})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\xi)+\epsilon\cdot d\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\xi)[h]+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\hat{\xi})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\xi)+\epsilon\cdot d\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\xi)[h]+\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{2}),

where, using the definition of the 1–mode initial data, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ij\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\cdot

\displaystyle\cdot\left[h^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}+\xi^{i}h^{j}\xi^{\kappa}+\xi^{i}\xi^{j}h^{\kappa}\right]\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{i\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}h^{i}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma j\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j})^{2}}h^{j}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}h^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)\Bigg\}.

Recall the definition of the eigenvalues
\ssomega_{i}:=i+2
for all integers
i\geq 0
and also recall that
m,i,j,\kappa,\nu,\gamma\geq 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}-\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{cases}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i=m\text{ and }\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\\
i=2\gamma-m\text{ and }m\leq 2\gamma\text{ and }\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\\
i=2\gamma+m+4\text{ and }\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}-\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}-\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{cases}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}j=2\gamma+m+4\text{ and }\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\\
j=2\gamma-m\text{ and }m\leq 2\gamma\text{ and }\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\\
j=m\text{ and }\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0\Longleftrightarrow\begin{cases}m=-\kappa-4\\
m=\kappa\end{cases}\Longleftrightarrow\kappa=m.

Therefore, we infer

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{-2}\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}h^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma-m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{2\gamma+m+4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma+m+4,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma+m+4}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{2\gamma+m+4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,2\gamma+m+4,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{2\gamma+m+4})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,2\gamma-m,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{2\gamma-m})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{m}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}.

The latter can be written as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{-2}\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}=h^{m}\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma-m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{2\gamma+m+4}\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma+m+4,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma+m+4}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right].

Similarly, using the definition of the 1–mode initial data, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\kappa,\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ij\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j})^{2}}\cdot

\displaystyle\cdot\left[h^{i}\xi^{j}\xi^{\kappa}+\xi^{i}h^{j}\xi^{\kappa}+\xi^{i}\xi^{j}h^{\kappa}\right]\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{i\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}h^{i}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu\geq 0}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\geq 0\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma j\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j})^{2}}h^{j}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa,\nu\geq 0\end{subarray}}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\kappa\nu m}\frac{\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu})\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}h^{\kappa}\sum_{\pm}\mathds{1}(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0)\Bigg\}.

As before, recall the definition of the eigenvalues
\ssomega_{i}:=i+2
for all integers
i\geq 0
and also recall that
m,i,j,\kappa,\nu,\gamma\geq 0
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{i}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{cases}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i=-4+m-2\gamma\text{ and }m\geq 4+2\gamma\text{ and }\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\\
i=m\text{ and }\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\pm\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}-\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}-\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{j}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{cases}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}j=-4+m-2\gamma\text{ and }m\geq 4+2\gamma\text{ and }\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\\
j=m\text{ and }\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\pm\ssomega_{\kappa}\pm\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\kappa}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\kappa}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{\kappa}+\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\\
\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}-\ssomega_{\kappa}-\ssomega_{m}=0\text{ and }\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma}\neq\pm\ssomega_{\nu}\end{cases}\Longleftrightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\kappa=-4+m-2\gamma\text{ and }m\geq 4+2\gamma\text{ and }\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\\
\kappa=4+m+2\gamma\text{ and }\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\\
\kappa=2\gamma-m\text{ and }m\leq 2\gamma\text{ and }\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{cases}

Therefore, we infer

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{-2}\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{9}{4}h^{-4+m-2\gamma}\mathds{1}(m\geq 4+2\gamma)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{-4+m-2\gamma,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{-4+m-2\gamma}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{-4+m-2\gamma}\mathds{1}(m\geq 4+2\gamma)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,-4+m-2\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{-4+m-2\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{m})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{-4+m-2\gamma}\mathds{1}(m\geq 4+2\gamma)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{-4+m-2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{4+m+2\gamma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{4+m+2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{9}{4}h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}.

The latter can be written as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{-2}\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}=h^{m}\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right]+h^{4+m+2\gamma}\left[\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{4+m+2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{-4+m-2\gamma}\mathds{1}(m\geq 4+2\gamma)\Bigg[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,-4+m-2\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{-4+m-2\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{-4+m-2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\left[\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right].

Putting all together, yields that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{-2}\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}=\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{-2}\left[\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0-}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}+\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0+}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}\right]

equals to

\displaystyle h^{m}\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\left[\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma-m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{4+m+2\gamma}\left[\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{4+m+2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma+m+4,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma+m+4}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{-4+m-2\gamma}\mathds{1}(m\geq 4+2\gamma)\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,-4+m-2\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{\gamma}+\ssomega_{-4+m-2\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{-4+m-2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right].

Finally, we simplify the formula above and show that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{4+m+2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}=\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma+m+4,\gamma,\nu}=0,

(2.28)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,-4+m-2\gamma,\nu}=\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{-4+m-2\gamma,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}=0,

(2.29)

for all
\gamma,\nu,m\geq 0
and
\gamma,\nu\geq 0
,
m\geq 2\gamma+4
respectively. These follow immediately from the fact that
\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}=0
for all integers
i,j,m\geq 0
with
i+j<m
(Lemma
5.8
). Specifically, we have

•

\nu>2\gamma\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}=0
,

•

0\leq\nu\leq 2\gamma\Longrightarrow m+\nu\leq m+2\gamma<4+m+2\gamma\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{4+m+2\gamma,\nu,m}=0
,

as well as

•

\nu>\gamma+m\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}=0
,

•

0\leq\nu\leq\gamma+m\Longrightarrow\gamma+\nu\leq 2\gamma+m<2\gamma+m+4\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma+m+4,\gamma,\nu}=0
,

that prove (
2.28
). Similarly, we have

•

m>\gamma+\nu\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}=0
,

•

0\leq m\leq\gamma+\nu\Longrightarrow-4+m-2\gamma+\gamma=-4+m-\gamma\leq-4+\nu<\nu\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma,-4+m-2\gamma,\nu}=0
,

as well as

•

\nu>2\gamma\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}=0
,

•

0\leq\nu\leq 2\gamma\Longrightarrow-4+m-2\gamma+\nu\leq-4+m<m\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{-4+m-2\gamma,\nu,m}=0
,

that prove (
2.29
). Therefore,
\mathfrak{K}_{\gamma}^{-2}\left(d\mathfrak{F}_{0}(\xi)[h]\right)^{m}
boils down to

\displaystyle h^{m}\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+m+\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\left[\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq 2+2\gamma\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma-m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right].

Using the same argument as above (Lemma
5.8
), we also infer

•

\nu>2\gamma\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}=0
,

•

\nu>\gamma+m\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}=0
.

Hence, the latter reduces to

\displaystyle h^{m}\left[\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\end{subarray}}^{m+\gamma}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}+\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{m+\gamma}\frac{\left(\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\gamma\nu}\right)^{2}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\end{subarray}}^{2\gamma}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{m\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+h^{2\gamma-m}\mathds{1}(0\leq m\leq 2\gamma)\left[\frac{9}{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\end{subarray}}^{2\gamma}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\nu,m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\gamma\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(2\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}+\frac{9}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=0\\
\nu\neq\pm(m-\gamma)-2\end{subarray}}^{m+\gamma}\frac{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{\gamma\nu m}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{2\gamma-m,\gamma,\nu}}{\ssomega_{\nu}^{2}-(\ssomega_{2\gamma-m}-\ssomega_{\gamma})^{2}}\right],

that completes the proof.
∎

3.
The linear eigenvalue problems

Next, we the study the linear eigenvalue problems where the linearized operators are given by (
1.12
). As it turns out, in all three models, the associated eigenfunctions are given by Jacobi polynomials and this is a common feature with the Einstein-Klein-Gordon system in spherical symmetry
[
27
]
.

3.1.
Conformal cubic wave equation in spherical symmetry

Consider the Hilbert space

\displaystyle L^{2}([0,\pi];\sin^{2}(x)dx)

(3.1)

associated with the inner product

\displaystyle(f|g):=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}f(x)g(x)\sin^{2}(x)dx.

For the conformal wave equation in spherical symmetry, the operator that governs the solutions to the linearized equation is given by

\displaystyle Lu

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{\sin^{2}(x)}\partial_{x}\left(\sin^{2}(x)\partial_{x}u\right)+u,

endowed with the domain
\mathcal{D}(L)
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(L):=\{u\in L^{2}((0,\pi);\sin^{2}(x)dx):\hskip 5.0ptLu\in L^{2}((0,\pi);\sin^{2}(x)dx)\}.

The operator
L
is generated by the closed sesquilinear form
a
defined on

\displaystyle H^{1}((0,\pi);\sin^{2}(x)dx)\times H^{1}((0,\pi);\sin^{2}(x)dx)

that is given by

\displaystyle a\left(u,v\right):=\int_{0}^{\pi}\left(\partial_{x}\overline{u}\cdot\partial_{x}v+\overline{u}\cdot v\right)\sin^{2}(x)dx,

where the bar denotes the complex conjugate, and

\displaystyle a(u,u)=\frac{2}{\pi}\|u\|_{H^{1}((0,\pi);\sin^{2}(x)dx)}^{2}.

In particular,
L
is self-adjoint on
\mathcal{D}(L)
. Now, the eigenvalue problem
Lu=\omega^{2}u
reads

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\left(\sin^{2}(x)\partial_{x}u\right)+(\omega^{2}-1)\sin^{2}(x)u=0

and by setting

\displaystyle u(x)=v(y),\hskip 5.0pty=\cos(x)

it becomes

\displaystyle(1-y^{2})v^{\prime\prime}(y)-3yv^{\prime}(y)+(\omega^{2}-1)v(y)=0

that has non–trivial solutions if and only if the solutions given by the Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind
[
38
]
, that is
v(y)=U_{n}(y)
, associated with the eigenvalues

\displaystyle\omega_{n}^{2}:=(n+1)^{2},\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0.

Hence, the solutions to the eigenvalue problem
Lu=\omega^{2}u
are given by

\displaystyle e_{n}(x):=U_{n}(\cos(x)),\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0.

(3.2)

In addition, the set
\{e_{n}:n\geq 0\}
forms an orthonormal and complete basis for the weighted
L^{2}
in (
3.1
). In fact, their orthogonality follows by the orthogonality of the Chebyshev polynomials of the second kind since

\displaystyle\left(e_{n}|e_{m}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}e_{n}(x)e_{m}(x)\sin^{2}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}U_{n}(y)U_{m}(y)(1-y^{2})\frac{dy}{\sqrt{1-y^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}U_{n}(y)U_{m}(y)\sqrt{1-y^{2}}dy

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}(n=m),

for any
n,m\geq 0
.

3.2.
Conformal cubic wave equation out of spherical symmetry

Consider the Hilbert space

\displaystyle L^{2}([0,\pi];\sin^{2}(x)dx)

(3.3)

associated with the inner product

\displaystyle\langle f|g\rangle:=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}f(x)g(x)\sin(2x)dx.

For the conformal cubic wave equation out of spherical symmetry, the operator that governs the solutions to the linearized equation is given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}u

\displaystyle:=-\partial_{x}^{2}u-\left(\frac{\cos x}{\sin x}-\frac{\sin x}{\cos x}\right)\partial_{x}u+\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{2}}{\sin^{2}x}+\frac{\mu_{2}^{2}}{\cos^{2}x}+1\right)u

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\sin(2x)}\partial_{x}\left(\sin(2x)\partial_{x}u\right)+\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{2}}{\sin^{2}x}+\frac{\mu_{2}^{2}}{\cos^{2}x}+1\right)u

endowed with the domain
\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{L^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}})
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{L^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}}):=\{u\in L^{2}((0,\pi/2);\sin(2x)dx):\hskip 5.0pt\mathsf{L}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}u\in L^{2}((0,\pi/2);\sin(2x)dx)\}.

The operator
\mathsf{L}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}
is generated by the closed sesquilinear form
\mathsf{a}
defined on

\displaystyle H^{1}((0,\pi/2);\sin(2x)dx)\times H^{1}((0,\pi/2);\sin(2x)dx)

that is given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}\left(u,v\right):=\int_{0}^{\pi}\left(\partial_{x}\overline{u}\cdot\partial_{x}v+\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{2}}{\sin^{2}x}+\frac{\mu_{2}^{2}}{\cos^{2}x}+1\right)\overline{u}\cdot v\right)\sin(2x)dx,

where the bar denotes the complex conjugate. and Hardy’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}(u,u)\simeq\|u\|_{H^{1}((0,\pi/2);\sin(2x)dx)}^{2}.

In particular,
\mathsf{L^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}}
is self-adjoint on
\mathcal{D}(\mathsf{L^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}})
. Now, the eigenvalue problem
\mathsf{L}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}u=\somega^{2}u
reads

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{2}u+\left(\frac{\cos x}{\sin x}-\frac{\sin x}{\cos x}\right)\partial_{x}u-\left(\frac{\mu_{1}^{2}}{\sin^{2}x}+\frac{\mu_{2}^{2}}{\cos^{2}x}+1-\omega^{2}\right)u=0.

and by setting

\displaystyle u(x)=v(y),\hskip 5.0ptv(y)=(1-y)^{\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}}(1+y)^{\frac{\mu_{2}}{2}}w(y),\hskip 5.0pty=\cos(2x)

it becomes

\displaystyle(1-y^{2})w^{\prime\prime}(y)+\left[(\mu_{2}-\mu_{1})-(2+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})y\right]w^{\prime}(y)+\frac{1}{4}\left[\omega^{2}-(1+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})^{2}\right]w(y)=0

that has non–trivial solutions if and only if the solutions are given by the Jacobi polynomial with parameters
(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
and degree
n\geq 0
, that is
w(y)=P_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(y)
, associated with the eigenvalues
associated with the eigenvalues

\displaystyle\left(\somega_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}\right)^{2}:=(2n+1+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2})^{2},\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0

Hence, the solutions to the eigenvalue problem
\mathsf{L}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}u=\somega^{2}u
are given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(x)=\mathsf{N}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(1-\cos(2x))^{\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}}(1+\cos(2x))^{\frac{\mu_{2}}{2}}P_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(\cos(2x)),\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0,

(3.4)

where the normalization constant reads

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}:=\sqrt{\frac{\somega_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}}{2^{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}}\frac{\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(n+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+1)}{\Gamma(n+\mu_{1}+1)\Gamma(n+\mu_{2}+1)}},\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0.

(3.5)

In addition, the set
\{\mathsf{e}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}:n\geq 0\}
forms an orthonormal and complete basis for the weighted
L^{2}
in (
3.3
). In fact, their orthogonality follows by the orthogonality of the Jacobi polynomials since

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{e}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}|\mathsf{e}_{m}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\mathsf{e}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(x)\mathsf{e}_{m}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(x)\sin(2x)dx

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{N}_{n}\mathsf{N}_{m}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-y)^{\mu_{1}}(1+y)^{\mu_{2}}P_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(y)P_{m}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(y)dy

\displaystyle=\mathsf{N}_{n}^{2}\frac{2^{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}}{2n+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+1}\frac{\Gamma(n+\mu_{1}+1)\Gamma(n+\mu_{2}+1)}{\Gamma(n+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}+1)\Gamma(n+1)}\mathds{1}(n=m)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}(n=m),

for any
n,m\geq 0
.

3.3.
Yang–Mills equation in spherical symmetry

Consider the Hilbert space

\displaystyle L^{2}([0,\pi];\sin^{4}(x)dx)

(3.6)

associated with the inner product

\displaystyle[f|g]:=\int_{0}^{\pi}f(x)g(x)\sin^{4}(x)dx.

For the Yang–Mills equation in spherical symmetry, the operator that governs the solutions to the linearized equation is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}u:=-\frac{1}{\sin^{4}x}\partial_{x}\left(\sin^{4}x\partial_{x}u\right)+4u

endowed with the domain
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L}):=\{u\in L^{2}((0,\pi);\sin^{4}xdx):\hskip 5.0pt\mathfrak{L}u\in L^{2}((0,\pi);\sin^{4}xdx)\}.

The operator
\mathfrak{L}
is generated by the closed sesquilinear form
\mathfrak{a}
defined on

\displaystyle H^{1}((0,\pi);\sin^{4}xdx)\times H^{1}((0,\pi);\sin^{4}xdx)

that is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}\left(u,v\right):=\int_{0}^{\pi}\left(\partial_{x}\overline{u}\cdot\partial_{x}v+4\overline{u}\cdot v\right)\sin^{4}(x)dx,

where the bar denotes the complex conjugate, and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}(u,u)\simeq\|u\|_{H^{1}((0,\pi);\sin^{4}xdx)}^{2}.

In particular,
\mathfrak{L}
is self-adjoint on
\mathcal{D}(\mathfrak{L})
. Now, the eigenvalue problem
\mathfrak{L}u=\ssomega^{2}u
reads

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{2}u+\frac{4}{\tan(x)}\partial_{x}u+(\omega^{2}-4)u=0

and by setting

\displaystyle u(x)=w(y),\hskip 5.0pty=\cos(x)

it becomes

\displaystyle\left(1-y^{2}\right)w^{\prime\prime}(y)-5yw^{\prime}(y)+\left(\omega^{2}-4\right)w(y)=0

that has non–trivial solutions if and only if the solutions given by the Jacobi polynomials with parameters
(3/2,3/2)
and degree
n
, that is
w(y)=P_{n}^{(3/2,3/2)}(y)
, associated with the eigenvalues

\displaystyle\ssomega_{n}^{2}:=(n+2)^{2},\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0.

Hence, the solutions to the eigenvalue problem
\mathfrak{L}u=\ssomega^{2}u
are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{n}(x):=\mathfrak{N}_{n}P_{n}^{\left(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right)}(\cos(x)),\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0,

(3.7)

where the normalization constant reads

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{n}:=\frac{\sqrt{\ssomega_{n}\Gamma(1+n)\Gamma(4+n)}}{2\sqrt{2}\Gamma\left(\frac{5}{2}+n\right)},\hskip 5.0ptn\geq 0.

(3.8)

In addition, the set
\{\mathfrak{e}_{n}:n\geq 0\}
forms an orthonormal and complete basis for the weighted
L^{2}
in (
3.6
). In fact, their orthogonality follows by the orthogonality of the Jacobi polynomials since

\displaystyle\left[\mathfrak{e}_{n}|\mathfrak{e}_{m}\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathfrak{e}_{n}(x)\mathfrak{e}_{m}(x)\sin^{4}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{N}_{n}\mathfrak{N}_{m}\int_{-1}^{1}P_{n}^{\left(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right)}(y)P_{m}^{\left(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right)}(y)\left(1-y^{2}\right)^{2}\frac{dy}{\sqrt{1-y^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{N}_{n}\mathfrak{N}_{m}\int_{-1}^{1}P_{n}^{\left(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right)}(y)P_{m}^{\left(\frac{3}{2},\frac{3}{2}\right)}(y)\left(1-y^{2}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}dy

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{N}_{n}^{2}\frac{2^{4}}{2n+4}\frac{\left(\Gamma\left(n+\frac{5}{2}\right)\right)^{2}}{\Gamma\left(n+4\right)\Gamma\left(n+1\right)}\mathds{1}(n=m)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}(n=m),

for all
n,m\geq 0
.

4.
The PDEs in Fourier space

In this section, we express the partial differential equations (
1.11
),

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}^{2}+\mathbf{L}\right)u=\mathbf{f}(x,u),\hskip 5.0pt(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times I,

in the Fourier space to obtain infinite dimensional systems of coupled, non-linear harmonic oscillators and we provide basic estimates for the non-linearities. Here, the non–linearities are given by (
1.13
), namely

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}(x,u)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-u^{3},&\text{ for CW}\\
-u^{3},&\text{ for WH}\\
-3u^{2}-\sin^{2}(x)u^{3},&\text{ for YM}\end{cases}

Let
u(t,\cdot)
be a solution to any of the three models,

•

CW: (
1.3
)–(
1.4
),

•

WH: (
1.6
)–(
1.7
) together with the ansatz (
1.5
),

•

YM: (
1.9
)–(
1.10
),

and recall that the set of the associated eigenfunctions,

•

CW:
\{e_{n}:n\geq 0\}
given by (
3.2
),

•

WH:
\{\mathsf{e}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}:n\geq 0\}
given by (
3.4
),

•

YM:
\{\mathfrak{e}_{n}:n\geq 0\}
given by (
3.7
),

forms an orthonormal and complete basis of the Hilbert spaces

•

CW:
L^{2}([0,\pi];\sin^{2}(x)dx)
,

•

WH:
L^{2}([0,\pi/2];\sin(2x)dx)
,

•

YM:
L^{2}([0,\pi];\sin^{4}(x)dx)
.

Then, we expand
u(t,\cdot)
in terms of the eigenfunctions and substitute the expression into (
1.11
) to find infinite systems of non–linear harmonic oscillators.

4.1.
Conformal cubic wave equation in spherical symmetry

For the conformal cubic wave equation in spherical symmetry, we expand

\displaystyle u(t,x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{n}(t)e_{n}(x),

and use the expansion

\displaystyle e_{i}e_{j}e_{k}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}C_{ijkm}e_{m}

(4.1)

to find the following infinite system of non–linear harmonic oscillators

\displaystyle\ddot{u}^{m}(t)+\left(Au(t)\right)^{m}=\left(f(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m},\hskip 5.0ptm\geq 0

(4.2)

where the dots denote derivatives with respect to time and

\displaystyle\left(Au(t)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=\omega_{m}^{2}u^{m}(t),

\displaystyle\left(f(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{i,j,k=0}^{\infty}C_{ijkm}u^{i}(t)u^{j}(t)u^{k}(t).

(4.3)

4.2.
Conformal cubic wave equation out of spherical symmetry

For the conformal cubic wave equation out of spherical symmetry, we expand

\displaystyle u(t,x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{n}(t)\mathsf{e}_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}(x),

and use the expansion

\displaystyle\mathsf{e}_{i}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}\mathsf{e}_{j}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}\mathsf{e}_{k}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}{}\mathsf{C}_{ijkm}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}\mathsf{e}_{m}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}

(4.4)

to find the following infinite system of non–linear harmonic oscillators

\displaystyle\ddot{u}^{m}(t)+\left(\mathsf{A}u(t)\right)^{m}=\left(\mathsf{f}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m},\hskip 5.0ptm\geq 0

(4.5)

where the dots denote derivatives with respect to time and

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{A}u(t)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=\left(\somega_{n}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}\right)^{2}u^{m}(t),

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{f}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{i,j,k=0}^{\infty}\mathsf{C}_{ijkm}^{(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}u^{i}(t)u^{j}(t)u^{k}(t).

(4.6)

4.3.
Yang–Mills equation in spherical symmetry

For the Yang–Mills equation in spherical symmetry, we expand

\displaystyle u(t,x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{n}(t)\mathfrak{e}_{n}(x),

and use the expansions

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{i}(x)\mathfrak{e}_{j}(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}\mathfrak{e}_{m}(x),\hskip 5.0pt\sin^{2}(x)\mathfrak{e}_{i}(x)\mathfrak{e}_{j}(x)\mathfrak{e}_{k}(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{C}_{ijkm}\mathfrak{e}_{m}(x)

(4.7)

to find the following infinite system of non–linear harmonic oscillators

\displaystyle\ddot{u}^{m}(t)+\left(\mathfrak{A}u(t)\right)^{m}=\left(\mathfrak{f}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m},\hskip 5.0ptm\geq 0

(4.8)

where the dots denote derivatives with respect to time and

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{A}u(t)\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=\ssomega_{m}^{2}u^{m}(t),

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{f}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m}+\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m},

with

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=-3\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}\overline{\mathfrak{C}}_{ijm}u^{i}(t)u^{j}(t),

(4.9)

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}(\{u^{j}(t):j\geq 0\})\right)^{m}

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{i,j,k=0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{C}_{ijkm}u^{i}(t)u^{j}(t)u^{k}(t).

(4.10)

4.4.
Lipschitz bounds

Recall Section
2
where we define the Banach space

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}\subseteq H^{k}([0,2\pi];l_{s}^{2})

consisting of spacetime functions of the form

\displaystyle q(t)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}q^{j}(t)e_{j}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q^{lj}\cos(lt)\right)e_{j}

such that the norm

\displaystyle\|q\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}^{2}:=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}j^{2s}\left(2|q^{0j}|^{2}+\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}|q^{lj}|^{2}(1+l^{2})^{k}\right)

is finite. Note that

\displaystyle\|q\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}^{2}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\sum_{\lambda=0}^{k}|q^{(\lambda)}(t)|_{s}^{2}dt,

where
q^{(\lambda)}(t)
denotes the
\lambda-
th derivative of
q(t)
with respect to
t
. Next, we show that the non–linear terms we consider satisfy the following Lipschitz bounds and we begin by considering the conformal cubic wave equation in spherical symmetry.

Lemma 4.1

(Lipschitz bounds for the CW)
.

Let
f
be given by (
4.3
). Then, for all integers
k\geq 0
and
s\geq 2
, there exists a positive constant (depending only on
k
and
s
) such that

\displaystyle\|f(u)-f(v)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}^{2}+\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}^{2}\right)\|u-v\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}},

\displaystyle\left\|df(u)[h]-df(v)[h]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}+\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\right)\left\|h\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\left\|u-v\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}},

for all
u,v,h\in\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}
with
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\leq\epsilon
,
\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\leq\epsilon
and
\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\leq\epsilon
.

Remark 4.2

(Regularity of the initial data for the CW model)
.

As stated above, for the CW model, we require
s\in\mathbb{N}
with
s\geq 2
. This means that the space of initial data
\mathcal{Q}\simeq l_{s+1}^{2}
is at least
l_{3}^{2}
(Theorem
2.4
).

Proof.

Let
s\geq 2
be an integer and pick any
u=\{u^{j}:j\geq 0\}\in l^{2}_{s}
. We also denote by
u
the corresponding function in the physical space, that is

\displaystyle u(x):=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}u_{j}e_{j}(x)

and recall that the operator in physical space is given by

\displaystyle Lu:=-\frac{1}{\sin^{2}(x)}\partial_{x}\left(\sin^{2}(x)\partial_{x}u\right)+u

On the one hand, for any integer
s\geq 1
, we define the Sobolev space
H^{s}_{CW}
space for spherically symmetric functions,

\displaystyle\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{\text{CW}}}:=\int_{0}^{\pi}uL^{s}u\sin^{2}xdx=\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\omega_{j}^{2s}|u_{j}|^{2}\simeq|u|_{s},

since
\omega_{j}\simeq j
. On the other hand, note that, with a slight abuse of notation (we denote by
u
the original variable as well as the spherically symmetric version of it), we have that the Sobolev space above is equivalent to the standard Sobolev on
\mathbb{S}^{3}
, defined by

\displaystyle\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}(\mathbb{S}^{3})}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{3}}u(-\Delta^{s}_{\mathbb{S}^{3}}u)d\text{vol}_{\mathbb{S}^{3}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{3})}^{2},

that is

\displaystyle\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}_{CW}}\simeq\|u\|^{2}_{H^{s}(\mathbb{S}^{3})}.

Here,
\Delta_{\mathbb{S}^{3}}
stands for the standard Laplacian on
\mathbb{S}^{3}
for the round metric and the standard volume form
d\text{vol}_{\mathbb{S}^{3}}
. This equivalence yields that
H^{s}_{CW}
is an algebra, since
H^{s}(\mathbb{S}^{3})
is an algebra provided that
s\geq 3/2
. Then, we pick an integer
s\geq 2
,
u,v\in l_{s}^{2}
and the algebra property, the triangular inequality together with Plancherel’s theorem yield

\displaystyle\left|f(u)\right|_{s}

\displaystyle=\left\|u^{3}\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}^{3}=\left|u\right|_{s}^{3},

\displaystyle\left|f(u)-f(v)\right|_{s}

\displaystyle=\left\|u^{3}-v^{3}\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}=\left\|(u-v)(u^{2}+uv+v^{2})\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|u-v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left(\left\|u^{2}\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}+\left\|uv\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}+\left\|v^{2}\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|u-v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left(\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}^{2}+\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left\|v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|u-v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left(\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left|u-v\right|_{s}\left(\left|u\right|_{s}^{2}+\left|v\right|_{s}^{2}\right),

\displaystyle\left|df(u)[h]-df(v)[h]\right|_{s}

\displaystyle=\left\|df(u)[h]-df(v)[h]\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}=\left\|d(u^{3})[h]-d(v^{3})[h]\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}

\displaystyle=\left\|3u^{2}h-3v^{2}h\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\lesssim\left\|u^{2}-v^{2}\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left\|h\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(u-v)(u+v)\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left\|h\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|u-v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left\|u+v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left\|h\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|u-v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\left(\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}+\left\|v\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}\right)\left\|h\right\|_{H^{s}_{CW}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|u-v\right|_{s}\left(\left|u\right|_{s}+\left|v\right|_{s}\right)\left|h\right|_{s}.

This proves the claim for
k=0
, Finally, we present the proof for
k=1
. In this case, Plancherel’s theorem yields

\displaystyle\left\|f(u)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}

\displaystyle=\left\|f(u)\right\|_{H^{1}_{t}l_{s}^{2}}=\left\|f(u)\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}l_{s}^{2}}+\left\|\partial_{t}f(u)\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}l_{s}^{2}}=\left\|f(u)\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}+\left\|\partial_{t}f(u)\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}.

Furthermore, the algebra property, Holder’s inequality together with the embedding
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
yield

\displaystyle\left\|f(u)\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\left\|u^{3}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}=\left\|\left\|u^{3}\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\lesssim\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}^{3}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\leq\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}^{2}\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\lesssim\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{H^{1}_{t}}^{2}\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\|u\|_{H^{1}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}^{2}\|u\|_{H^{0}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\leq\|u\|_{H^{1}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}^{3},

\displaystyle\left\|\partial_{t}f(u)\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}

\displaystyle=\left\|3u^{2}\partial_{t}u\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}\simeq\left\|\left\|u^{2}\partial_{t}u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\lesssim\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}^{2}\left\|\partial_{t}u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}\left\|\left\|\partial_{t}u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}=\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}^{2}\left\|\left\|\partial_{t}u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{H^{1}_{t}}^{2}\left\|\left\|u\right\|_{H^{s}_{x}}\right\|_{H^{1}_{t}}\leq\left\|u\right\|_{H^{1}_{t}H^{s}_{x}}^{3}

and hence

\displaystyle\left\|f(u)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}\lesssim\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}_{s}}^{3}.

All the other bounds follow similarly.
∎

Next, we consider the conformal cubic wave equation out of spherical symmetry.

Lemma 4.3

(Lipschitz bounds for the CH)
.

Let
\mathsf{f}
be given by (
4.6
). Then, for all integers
k\geq 0
and
s\geq 2
, there exists a positive constant (depending only on
k
and
s
) such that

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{f}(u)-\mathsf{f}(v)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}^{2}+\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}^{2}\right)\|u-v\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}},

\displaystyle\left\|d\mathsf{f}(u)[h]-d\mathsf{f}(v)[h]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\left\|u\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}+\left\|v\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\right)\left\|h\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\left\|u-v\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}},

for all
u,v,h\in\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}
with
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\leq\epsilon
,
\|v\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\leq\epsilon
and
\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}^{k}_{s}}\leq\epsilon
.

Remark 4.4

(Regularity of the initial data for the CH model)
.

As stated above, for the CH model, we require
s\in\mathbb{N}
with
s\geq 2
. This means that the space of initial data
\mathcal{Q}\simeq l_{s+1}^{2}
